Together, these propositions make it physically possible for a shooter located in the main shop (where Nash was) to fire through the door into the rear storeroom and hit Windibank from behind.  

Thus, the existence of a fired gun (Evidence 13) combined with the spatial layout (Props 2 & 4) contradicts Nash’s claim that they “never even had a chance” to shoot.

**Step 4:**  
Even without the listed propositions, the simple spatial reasoning is that the victim was in the rear storeroom, the door connects the main shop to that storeroom, and the brothers were in the main shop. Therefore they were in a position to fire the shot, making Nash’s denial contradictory.

**Answer (the contradictory pair):**  
{"evidence": 13, "testimony": 6}

**Step 4:** Thus the spatial reasoning shows a direct conflict: the physical evidence (left‑hand prints) contradicts the witness’s description of how the defendant handled the bottle.
